# Dead code in the Elm compiler: full audit (2026-11-04)

## How this was measured

1. **Coverage.** `compiler/scripts/coverage/cov.py setup && cov.py baseline` instrumented every module
   under `compiler/src`, `src-xhr` and `src-version` with elm-coverage and ran both suites against it:
   elm-test-rs (21,362 passed, 0 failed) and the JIT E2E suite through an instrumented `guida.js`
   (2,594 / 2,594). Result in `build/coverage/baseline/`. 14,880 of 45,463 annotations were hit by
   neither suite; 3,331 of those are behind default-off flags (`flag` rows in
   `compiler/coverage/exclusions.csv`) and are **not** dead, so they are out of scope here.
2. **Reachability from the shipped entry points.** Stock `elm make` of `Terminal.Main` and `API.Main`
   (Elm's tree shaking is exact for top-level values): 49 of 7,157 top-level declarations are absent,
   and one whole module (`Compiler.MonoOpt.Borrow.Facts`) is unreachable.
3. **elm-review `NoUnused.*`**, unsuppressed, once over the whole project and once with `tests/` hidden
   (to find exports kept alive only by tests).
4. **Classification.** The ~11,500 remaining unhit annotations, the 552 existing `unreachable`
   exclusion rows and the crash branches were read by eight area audits. Each unhit annotation ended
   up as a dead item (with an argument: `unref`, `types`, `callers` or `invariant`), a
   claimed-unreachable-but-reachable finding, or an "unhit but live" cluster. Unhit alone was never
   taken as evidence.

Limits: coverage cannot see code that only tests reach at branch level (top-level is covered by
step 2), and `invariant` arguments are only as strong as the invariant they cite; those items say so.

## Totals

433 dead items (each groups every annotation that dies for the same reason).

| Proposed fix | Items |
|---|---|
| 1. Delete, unused | 115 |
| 2. Restructure so the state is unrepresentable | 177 |
| 3. Delete and return a default | 71 |
| 4. Delete and crash (new crash) | 47 |
| Keep the existing crash (no better option found) | ~15 |
| Mixed / per-site within one item | ~8 |

Per area: format 34, parse 6, JS backend + docs 22, MLIR codegen + MonoOpt 136, Monomorphize +
PreMono 113, front end (type checker, canonicalize, LocalOpt, reporting) 83, IR + Builder.Deps/Eco 22,
CLI / Builder / API / utilities 17.

## Act on these first: code claimed unreachable that is reachable

These are bugs or wrong exclusion reasons, not dead code. Details and inputs are in the area sections.

| Where | What reaches it | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `Type/Type.elm:1017` (crash) | `f : { a \| x : Int } -> a -> Int` / `f r y = (y + 1) + (r + 1)`; **reproduced** | compiler crash instead of two type errors |
| `IR/Runtime/CtorTag.elm:131-138` `checkNullConsCapacity` (crash) | a custom type with 1025 or more constructors that uses a nullary one at index 1024 or above | internal crash; should be a reported limit error |
| `API/Make.elm:105-106` `crash "No main!"` | API `diagnostics`/`make` on a module without `main` | API process crashes instead of returning JSON error |
| `Builder/Build.elm:3030` `addInside` `RNotFound` (crash) | `src/Html.elm` in an app depending on elm/html, `eco make src/Html.elm` (found by reading) | crash instead of ambiguous-import error |
| `Builder/Build.elm:3002, 3036` `RBlocked`/`ROutsideBlocked` (crash) | a corrupted or deleted cached `.eci` | crash; should be a "corrupt cache" error |
| `GenerateJs/Expression.elm:479-484` `generateField` (crash) | `--optimize` JS with a shader attribute or uniform name that no record ever uses (found by reading) | crash |
| `Parse/Kernel.elm:366-441` (5 crashes), `Canonicalize/Environment/Foreign.elm:154` | kernel package/app with malformed kernel JS imports or `import Elm.Kernel.X as Y` | crash instead of a syntax error (kernel authors only) |
| `Type/Error.elm:642, 3013, 3082, 1429`; `Type/PostSolve.elm:1023-1145` | kernel-project-only inputs (effect module `init` mismatch, 0-arity infix, record-alias ctor on kernel ref) | no crash; exclusion rows are wrong, delete them |
| `Monomorphize/ResolveAccessorValues.elm:231`, `Solver/Monomorphize.elm:4651`, `Solver/Translate.elm:1147, 1217`, `Solver/Zonk.elm:147` | see b5 section (two verified with the instrumented compiler) | behaviour correct (except Translate 1147: alias-typed port encoder is not connected); exclusion reasons wrong |

Progress (2026-11-05):

- **Fixed:** corrupted `.eci` (Build.elm:3002, 3036) is now `Exit.BuildCorruptCache`, which gives the
  CORRUPT CACHE report. This covers an inside root, a first outside root and a later outside root (that one
  used to be silently left out of the output), and a package build from exposed modules (that path is untested). Pinned by three
  tests in `test/scripts/cli-builder.test.js`.
- **Fixed:** a nullary constructor past index 1023 (CtorTag.elm:131) is rejected at canonicalization with
  TOO MANY CONSTRUCTORS (`HeapLimits.maxNullaryCtorIndex`, HEAP_044(b) amended). The emission crash remains
  as a backstop. Pinned by `SourceIR.LimitFailsCases` "Nullary constructor positions" and a
  `LimitBoundaryCases` control.
- **Exclusions deleted** (reachable): Type/Error 642, 3013, 3082, 1429; PostSolve 1023-1093, 1112,
  1127-1145; ResolveAccessorValues 231; Solver/Monomorphize 4527 (fuel); Translate 1147, 1217; Zonk 147.
  PostSolve 1118 stays (dead per T27) with its reason corrected. Translate 1147's alias-typed port encoder
  is still not connected.
- **Fixed:** kernel imports (Parse/Kernel.elm:366-441, Canonicalize/Environment/Foreign.elm:154). Every
  kernel-file problem is now a `Compiler.Parse.Error.Kernel` syntax error: the five header forms that used to
  crash, a header that does not parse, and an unknown `__Tag`. `import Elm.Kernel.X as Y` in an Elm module of
  a kernel project is rejected by the parser, and Foreign's crash is gone. Builder/Build.elm:1008 no longer
  accepts a kernel file that does not parse (it gives `SBadSyntax`), and Builder/Deps/Details.elm reports one
  like any dependency compile error (`SBadKernel`, shown with `--Xpackage-errors`). Pinned by
  `tests/Compiler/Parse/KernelTest.elm`, `ModuleTest.elm` and 10 tests in `test/scripts/cli-errors.test.js`.
- **Fixed** (2026-11-05), each after a failing test:
  - Type.elm:1017: a record extension in the `Error` or `Infinite` state is shown closed instead of
    crashing (`SourceIR/TypeCheckFailsCases` "record whose extension an earlier error marked").
  - API/Make.elm "No main!": `make` answers `Exit.MakeNoMain` as JSON, and `diagnostics` answers `{}`
    because the module compiled.
  - Build.elm ambiguous root: `eco make src/Html.elm` next to elm/html gives AMBIGUOUS MODULE NAME
    (`Exit.BP_RootNameAmbiguous`, `checkAmbiguousRoots`).
  - GenerateJs generateField: the erased optimizer registers shader attribute and uniform names as fields.
  - NumList2, which was broader than reported: **any** let-bound `number` used at both `Int` and `Float`
    failed in the solver engine, even `n = 5`. Every use carries the binding's own type variables. Now a
    let-bound number used as a call argument gets a fresh copy of its type (`StashNumberMulti`, kernel
    calls included, which also unify their result first). Lists refine through head and tail paths
    (`replaceListSlot`), and a defaulted compound slot is read from the root's instance at its type
    (`refinedSlotRoot`). Pinned by `LetNumberListCaseTest` and `LetNumberDualUseTest`; all 53
    `LetNumber*` E2E tests pass.
  - Translate 1147 is not a bug: `test/elm/src/PortAliasEchoTest.elm` (an alias-typed port with a record
    payload) passes natively and pins that.

Side finding, found during the audit but not dead code: the default compiler fails with
`Kernel signature mismatch for Elm_Kernel_Basics_mul_Float` on a let-bound number list destructured
in a `case` and used at both Int and Float (repro kept at
`/tmp/deadcode/scratch/b5-mono-premono/port1/src/NumList2.elm`; copy it into a test before /tmp is cleared).
Also: the claim at `Reify.elm:1260` in `plans/dead-code-claims-tidy-up.md` is stale (already fixed).

## Reproducing

- Coverage: `python3 compiler/scripts/coverage/cov.py setup` once, then `cov.py baseline` (~21 min).
- Unhit dump over all modules, entry-point reachability and batching scripts used for this audit:
  `/tmp/deadcode/scripts/{dump,unreach,batch}.py` (ephemeral).

## Area: b1-format
Summary: 34 dead items (option 1: 10, option 2: 19, option 3: 5, option 4: 0), 0 claimed-unreachable-but-reachable (1 latent hazard noted).

Reachability: every `Common.Format.*` module is live. `API.Main` (`FormatArgs`, src/API/Main.elm:91) calls
`API.Format.run`, which calls `Common.Format.format` (src/API/Format.elm:31). `Terminal.Main` has no format command.
`unreachable-decls.csv` and `elm-review.csv` are empty for this batch: every top-level declaration is in the shipped
`API.Main` JS. Nearly all of the 1,676 unhit rows are untested, not dead. The items below are the parts inside the
modules that no input can reach. Line numbers are the unhit.csv rows (the expression after the pattern).

### Claimed-unreachable but reachable
None in the shipped compiler. All 14 `crash` rows (Box 179, 200; Inlines 454; Parse 513, 660, 1003, 1040, 1089, 1459;
Util 208; RWS 169; Render.Box 1246, 1964; ElmStructure 135) are unreachable for the reasons in the table.

Latent hazard (not reachable today): Parse.elm:1459 depends on JS `String.toInt` never overflowing. Eco's native
`StringOps::toInt` (runtime/src/allocator/StringOps.hpp:1195) returns `Nothing` for more than 19 digits. If `API.Main`
were ever built natively, a doc comment line starting with 20 digits would crash `format`. Fix #24 removes this.

### Unhit but live (not dead)
- Common.Format / API.Format: `format`, `render`. The `format` JSON command of API.Main is reachable but has no tests at all.
- Box: all line/box primitives, `render`, `renderRow_`, tab arithmetic, `addSuffix`, `mapFirstLine`. Reached by any format call.
- Render.Box header: `formatModuleHeader` (default header, docs-ordered exports, `sortVars`, `removeDuplicates`), `formatModuleLine` (port/effect tags, `where` settings incl. Fx sorting), `formatImports`/`formatImport` (all 11 single/multi-line layouts, alias suppression). Reached by modules with or without a header, comments and `@docs`.
- Render.Box `formatModu`/`declToDeclarations`: all Decl kinds (Value/Union/Alias/Port), doc comments, infixes (package elm/core allows them), effect-manager `Cmd`/`Sub`/`Fx`.
- Render.Box body/doc comments: `formatTopLevelBody` spacer cases other than #4/#34, `formatDocComment` (Declarations/Expressions/Module code blocks), `formatDocCommentString`, `lines`.
- Render.Box declarations, patterns, expressions, types: every remaining `formatDeclaration`/`formatPattern`/`formatExpression`/`formatType` branch, plus if/let/case/lambda/record/tuple/shader layouts, `negativeCasePatternWorkaround` (reachable via a hex literal pattern such as `0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF`, which is -1), `formatString` escapes, `formatLiteral` hex case, `formatComment` block/line, `formatUnit`, comment combinators. Reached by ordinary source.
- ElmStructure: all other layout combinators (`application` FASplitFirst/SplitAll, `equalsPair` MustBreak, `prefixOrIndented`, `spaceSepOrPrefix` <4-col prefix, `extensionGroup_`). Reached by multi-line or commented source.
- Render.Markdown: all block/inline printers, `needsInitialBlanks` cases, numbered/bullet/tight/loose lists, fenced and indented code, autolinks, nested links (the `fixSpecialChars=False` path), references, `longestSpanOf`. Reached by doc comments.
- Cheapskate.Parse: container continuation/start, verbatim containers, lazy continuation, list-item blank-line handling, ATX/setext/HR leaves, `blockHtmlTags`, list marker width, `( "??", "??", "??" )` reference fallback (reachable through nested containers). Reached by Markdown doc comments.
- Cheapskate.Inlines: HTML tags/comments, link labels/URLs/titles, references, emphasis `pOne`/`pTwo`/`pThree`, code spans, images, entities, autolinks/e-mail, bare URIs, `schemes`. Reached by doc-comment text.
- Cheapskate.ParserCombinators / Util / RWS: the remaining combinators and scanners. They are only used by the Markdown parser and are reached through it. `oneOf` error merging and `comparePositions` run but feed only discarded errors.
- ImportInfo: `fromModule`/`fromImports` are live. Note: `ImportInfo` is a field-less placeholder threaded through every formatter, which makes it a no-effect cleanup candidate but not dead.

## Area: b2-parse
Summary: 6 dead items (option 1: 3, option 2: 2, option 3: 1, option 4: 0), 1 claimed-unreachable-but-reachable (5 crash rows in Parse.Kernel).

Method: every constructor of every type in `Compiler.Parse.Error` was searched for producers (qualified `E.`/`Syntax.` uses in `src/` and `src-xhr/`, unqualified non-pattern uses in Error.elm above the codecs, comments and strings stripped). Only 7 constructors have no producer outside their own decoder (items 3 and 4). Scratch: `/tmp/deadcode/scratch/b2-parse/ctors2.tsv` (producer count per constructor), `groups.tsv` (unhit rows grouped by function).

### Dead code
| # | Location | What | Why dead (evidence) | Fix | Proposal |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | src/Compiler/Parse/Expression.elm:436-460 | `accessibleRecord` and `chompRecordAccessField` (all 9 unhit rows) | unref: in unreachable-decls; they only call each other, and their doc comments say nothing calls them. No test uses them. | 1 | Delete both. |
| 2 | src/Compiler/Parse/Declaration.elm:265-272 and src/Compiler/Parse/Expression.elm:1389-1396 | `chompMatchingName` (both copies): the `P.Eok` arm (both if-sides, rows 265/266/269 and 1389/1390/1393) and the `P.Cerr r c t` arm (272, 1396) | types/callers: the wrapped parser is `Var.lower` (Variable.elm:79-106), which only ever returns `Cok` or `Eerr`. The `Cok`-mismatch (262/1386) and `Eerr` (275/1399) arms are live (name mismatch after an annotation, or a non-name on the next line). | 2 | Replace the case over the raw `Step` with combinators: `P.getPosition` -> `Var.lower toRepeatErr` -> `P.getPosition` -> if equal `succeed (A.At (Region start end) name)` else a parser that returns `Cerr start.row start.col (toMatchErr name)`. The impossible arms then go away. Make it one shared helper, parameterised by `toRepeatErr`/`toMatchErr`, so the two copies are not duplicated. |
| 3 | src/Compiler/Parse/Error.elm:928,930-934 (type), 4327-4414 (toNumberReport branches), 11510-11531 (numberEncoder tags 3,5-9), 11553-11571 (numberDecoder) | `Number` constructors `NumberBinDigit`, `NumberNoLeadingOrTrailingUnderscores`, `NumberNoConsecutiveUnderscores`, `NumberNoUnderscoresAdjacentToDecimalOrExponent`, `NumberNoUnderscoresAdjacentToHexadecimalPreFix`, `NumberNoUnderscoresAdjacentToBinaryPreFix` | types: no producer anywhere in src/ or src-xhr (only the decoder builds them). `Parse.Number` has no binary-literal or digit-separator syntax: `0b` and `_` are never scanned. These are left over from Guida's number syntax. Tests only mention them in test names (NumberTest.elm:116-131 checks that `_` is rejected, through other errors). | 1 | Delete the 6 constructors, their 6 report branches, and their encoder and decoder arms, then renumber the tags (`NumberInexactOnHost` goes from 10 to 4). The codec only crosses MVars (Build/Details status and result values); no persisted cache user was found, so renumbering is safe. |
| 4 | src/Compiler/Parse/Error.elm:712 (type), 7303-7329 (toPatternReport branch), 10019-10027 (patternEncoder tag 10), 10119-10121 (patternDecoder) | `Pattern.PWildcardReservedWord` | types: no producer in src/ or src-xhr. `Pattern.wildcard` (Pattern.elm:150-170) only ever builds `PWildcardNotVar`, also for `_if` and similar. | 1 | Delete the constructor, its report branch and its codec arms, and renumber the later pattern tags (MVar-only codec, as in #3). Alternatively, if the friendlier "reserved word after `_`" message is wanted, make `wildcard` produce it when `Identifier.isReservedWord` holds. That would be a feature change, not dead-code removal. |
| 5 | src/Compiler/Parse/Shader.elm:272-273 | `addInput` `_ -> Crash.crash "Should never happen..."` (excluded=crash) | callers: the only caller folds over `extractInputs` (Shader.elm:180). Its single producing branch (293-311) is guarded by `List.member qual [Attribute, Varying, Uniform]`, so no other qualifier reaches `addInput`. | 2 | Make `extractInputs` return a closed local type, e.g. `type InputKind = Attr \| Unif \| Vary`, mapping the qualifier once (`Attribute -> [Attr ..]`, `Uniform -> ...`, `Varying -> ...`, `_ -> []`, which replaces the `List.member` guard). `addInput` then matches 3 constructors exhaustively and the crash goes. |
| 6 | src/Compiler/Parse/Shader.elm:206-207 | `parseGlsl` col: `_ -> 0` arm of `case List.reverse lines of` | types (library contract): `String.lines` never returns `[]` (JS split gives `[""]` for `""`; the native kernel `elm-kernel-cpp/src/core/String.cpp:180-195` returns a one-element list for null or empty input). | 3 | Compute `col = List.foldl (\l _ -> String.length l) 0 lines` (the last line's length). The default 0 is never observed, and it equals the column for an empty prefix anyway. |

### Claimed-unreachable but reachable
| Location | Crash | Input that reaches it |
|---|---|---|
| src/Compiler/Parse/Kernel.elm:366, 407, 419, 438, 441 (`addImport`, `toPrefix`, `toNames`, `toName`) | `crash "cannot use `as` with kernel import ..."`, `"kernel imports with dots need an alias"`, `"cannot have exposing (..)"`, `"cannot have Maybe(..) syntax"`, `"cannot use binops"` | A local project whose name passes `Pkg.isKernel` (author `elm`, `elm-explorations` or `eco`, e.g. an `elm.json` with `"type": "package", "name": "elm/foo"`), with `src/Elm/Kernel/Foo.js` whose header comment holds e.g. `import Elm.Kernel.Utils as U`, `import Json.Decode exposing (..)`, `import Maybe exposing (Maybe(..))` or `import Basics exposing ((+))`. `Build.checkKernelExistsInDirs` (Build.elm:1008) and `Details.parseAndCrawlKernel` (Details.elm:2227) parse it and the compiler aborts. The fromByteString doc comment documents this, so it is not an author mistake. But it is a user-reachable crash, and `fromByteString` already returns `Maybe`. Proposal: thread failure out of `toVarTable` (return `Maybe VarTable`/`Result`) and fail the parser (`Nothing`) instead of crashing. |

### Unhit but live (not dead)
- Parse.Error report functions (`toReport` through `toTTupleReport`, lines 970-8812, about 1,000 rows, including helpers `toRegion`, `toKeywordRegion`, `getDefName`, `isWithin`): every remaining constructor has a real parser producer. The branches are reachable by malformed source; no test renders a syntax-error report.
- Parse.Error codecs (`errorEncoder`/`errorDecoder` and every per-type encoder/decoder, lines 8845-12715, about 600 rows, minus the dead arms in #3 and #4): reachable but host-dependent. They run only when a syntax error crosses an MVar under the src-xhr host (`compiler/src-xhr/Eco/MVar.elm:90` encodes). The native host's `eco-kernel-cpp/src/Eco/MVar.elm:70` ignores the encoder, so the instrumented native compiler never runs them. Possibly worth a cross-batch note.
- `Error.NumberInexactOnHost` report (4416) and its codec arm: produced only by the JS-hosted compiler (Number.elm:177).
- Parse.Kernel (whole module except the crash rows above): kernel `.js` parsing for kernel packages and projects (JS backend and package crawl); not covered by tests.
- Parse.Declaration `infix_` (508-569), Parse.Module `chompInfixes` (507), Parse.Number `precedence` (550-568): infix declarations, which only kernel packages (elm/core sources) use.
- Parse.Module `checkModule` headerless branch (281-297), `checkEffects` error branches (321-362), effect-module exposing and `chompManager` command+subscription form (833, 929-941): headerless modules, port/effect misuse, and effect managers with both cmd and sub.
- Parse.Number error arms (203 `String.toFloat` Nothing: reachable natively, since the kernel `toFloat` returns Nothing for literals longer than 127 chars; 290/302 `1.`; 344-372 bad exponent; 428 leading zero): malformed literals.
- Parse.Pattern `wildcard` `_foo` error (163-164) and empty record pattern `{}` (206): rare or malformed syntax.
- Parse.Shader `EndlessShader`, `Unending`, GLSL parse-failure positioning, `showErrorMessages` (an empty message list from elm-combine is not provably impossible), `failure`, `extractInputs` fallthroughs (311 unsupported type, 314 non-input qualifier): malformed or unusual GLSL.
- Parse.Space tab and endless-comment errors, `\r`, a line comment at EOF, a `{` near EOF, nested block comments (105-108, 197-203, 277-353, 380-402, 483-486): malformed or rare whitespace.
- Parse.String char and string errors, `\r` and `\u{...}` in multi-line strings, escape at EOF, bad escapes (81-144, 287-410, 451-515): malformed or rare literals.
- Parse.Variable `Eerr`/`Bad` arms (61, 98, 132, 153, 182): missing names, reserved words, `Foo.` with no upper-case name after it.
- `chompMatchingName` `Cok`-mismatch (Declaration 262, Expression 1386) and `Eerr` (275, 1399): an annotation followed by a different or missing name.

## Area: b3-js-docs
Summary: 22 dead items (1: 2, 2: 12, 3: 2, 4: 6 [5 of them "keep crash"]), 1 claimed-unreachable-but-reachable.

Reachability baseline. The shipped entry points reach every module in this batch:
- JS backend (`Compiler.GenerateJs.*`): `eco make --output=x.js|x.html` (and the default `index.html`), through
  `Terminal/Make.elm:356-406` and `API/Make.elm:216-227`. It runs in three modes: dev, `--optimize` (Prod, short
  field names, `Nitpick.Debug`) and `--debug` (Dev+types, `Docs.Type.Extract` Msg metadata). `--sourcemaps` is a
  real flag (`Terminal/Main.elm:217`), so `SourceMap` is reached too.
- Docs: `eco make --docs` / no-path builds of a package (`Terminal/Make.elm:626-660`), `eco diff`/`bump`
  (`Terminal/Diff.elm:401`), and the docs of every dependency package that `Details` builds (`Builder/Deps/Details.elm:2464`).
- Json.*: reading and writing elm.json, docs.json, the registry and eco config. Text.PrettyPrint: every coloured terminal report.
- No test exercises the JS backend (E2E goes through MLIR), so nearly every row in GenerateJs is unhit, yet nearly
  all of it is live. `unreachable-decls.csv` and `elm-review.csv` are empty for this batch, so every top-level
  declaration is referenced from production. The dead code is unread record fields, or constructors and branches
  that no producer can reach.

### Dead code
| # | Location | What | Why dead (evidence) | Fix | Proposal |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | src/Compiler/GenerateJs/Generate.elm:200-355 (`generateForRepl`, `print`, `generateForReplEndpoint`, `postMessage`); src/Compiler/GenerateJs.elm:39-57 (lambdas l.40, l.50); src/Compiler/GenerateJs/CodeGen.elm:79-114 (fields `generateForRepl`, `generateForReplEndpoint`) | REPL code generation | unref: the fields `.generateForRepl` / `.generateForReplEndpoint` are written only in `GenerateJs.backend` and never read anywhere in src/ (grep). Neither Terminal.Main nor API.Main has a REPL command. No tests use them. Also orphans `Name.replValueToPrint` (src/Compiler/Core/Name.elm:687, outside this batch) and Generate's `RT`/`L` imports. | 1 | Delete the two record fields, the two lambdas and the four functions. `CodeGen` then has a single field, so `CodeGen` / `Generate.javascriptBackend` can collapse to a plain function (and `Output` has one constructor, `TextOutput`, so it could become `String`). |
| 2 | src/Compiler/GenerateJs/Expression.elm:292-294 (`Opt.Tuple _ a b cs`, `_ ->` TupleN); Expression.elm:1323-1324 (`Opt.ArrayIndex` in `generatePath`); src/Compiler/Nitpick/PatternMatches.elm:129-130 (`Can.PTuple a b cs` → `nTuple`); also the non-annotated `// EXTRA GUIDA CORE` `_Utils_TupleN` / `_Debug_toAnsiString` patch in Generate.elm:120-136 | Support for tuples of more than 3 elements (inherited from guida) | invariant: the canonicalizer throws `TupleLargerThanThree` for expressions (Canonicalize/Expression.elm:456), patterns (Canonicalize/Pattern.elm:355) and types (Canonicalize/Type.elm:136, which covers port tuples). So `cs` has length 0 or 1. The only producers of `Opt.ArrayIndex` are the `cs`-length≥2 arms of LocalOpt/Erased/Expression.elm:487-510 and Erased/Port.elm:231/486 (other batches). The MLIR backend already crashes here (GenerateMlir/Expr.elm:6972). | 2 | Change the third tuple component from `List` to `Maybe` in `Can.PTuple`/`Can.Tuple`/`Opt.Tuple` (or drop `Opt.ArrayIndex` and the 4+ arms). Then delete the TupleN arm, the ArrayIndex arm, the `nTuple` arm, the `_Utils_TupleN` prelude JS and the matching producer arms. If the IR change is too wide, fall back to crash arms (4). |
| 3 | src/Compiler/GenerateJs/Builder.elm:453-456 (`Break Nothing`), 466-469 (`Continue Nothing`) | Unlabelled break/continue printing | unref: the only producers are `JS.Break (Just ...)` (Expression.elm:1475) and `JS.Continue (Just ...)` (Expression.elm:1278). | 2 | Change `Break (Maybe Name)` / `Continue (Maybe Name)` to `Break Name` / `Continue Name` and delete the `Nothing` arms. |
| 4 | Builder.elm:724 (`ExprBool` "false"), Builder.elm:997 (`fromExprLines` `ExprBool`); Expression.elm:967 (`isLiteral` `ExprBool`), 1164-1168 and 1186-1190 (`strictEq` `ExprBool` arms), 1217-1221 and 1239-1243 (`strictNEq` `ExprBool` arms) | Handling of `JS.ExprBool` as an operand | callers/unref: `JS.ExprBool` is produced only as `JS.ExprBool True` for `while (true)` loop conditions (Expression.elm:1305, 1459). The loop condition is printed by `fromExpr` directly. It is never an operand of `strictEq`/`strictNEq`/`isLiteral` (Elm Bool literals become `ExprTrackedBool`, which stays live), never in a lines context, and never `False`. | 2 | Replace `While Expr Stmt` with `WhileTrue Stmt` (printing `while (true)`) and delete the `ExprBool` constructor together with all these arms. Minimal version (1): delete the `ExprBool` arms in `isLiteral`/`strictEq`/`strictNEq`, which fall through to `_` anyway. |

There `func` has a function type and is neither `Accessor` nor `Call` (those are matched earlier). Literals, enum constructors, lists, records, tuples, unit, shaders and updates cannot have a function type, and a `TailCall` cannot be an operand. | 3 | Keep the reachable arms (VarLocal, TrackedVarLocal, VarGlobal, VarBox, VarCycle, VarDebug, VarKernel, Function, TrackedFunction, If, Let, Destruct, Case, Access) and add `_ -> Nothing`. This is safe because the result only picks the source-map position of a synthesized call, which already defaults to `A.zero`. |
| 9 | Expression.elm:633-634 (`Opt.VarBox`, Prod, `_ ->`) | Prod call of a box constructor with ≠1 argument | types: a box constructor (`Can.Unbox`: one constructor, one field) takes exactly one argument and returns a non-function custom type. So a type-correct `Opt.Call` on it has exactly one argument. | 2 | Restructure as `case ( mode, args ) of ( Mode.Prod _, [ arg ] ) -> generateJsExpr ..; _ -> generateCallHelp ..`. The remaining `_` is the live Dev path. |

Or narrow the `opts` carried by `Test.IsCtor` to a two-valued tag representation (2, touches DT modules). |

Bool patterns become `Can.PBool` → `Test.IsBool`, never `IsCtor` (Canonicalize/Pattern.elm:287; DecisionTree.elm:438). This is a leftover from upstream Elm. | 1 | Delete the `if` and keep its else branch. |

| Location | Claim | Input that reaches it |
|---|---|---|
| src/Compiler/GenerateJs/Expression.elm:479-484 (`generateField`, Prod: `crash "field not found in ShortFieldNames"`) | Every field name generated in Prod mode is in the field-frequency table | `Opt.Shader` attribute and uniform names are generated with `generateField` (Expression.elm:297-308). However, `LocalOpt/Erased/Expression.elm:310-311` builds `Opt.Shader` without `Names.registerField*`, so a shader input name that no Elm record literal, access or update mentions is missing from `Mode.shortenFieldNames`. Example: `eco make --optimize --output=x.js` on an app (with elm-explorations/webgl) that has `vs : Shader { zzAttr : Vec3 } {} {}` defined as `[glsl| attribute vec3 zzAttr; void main(){ gl_Position = vec4(zzAttr,1.0); } |]` and draws `WebGL.entity vs fs (WebGL.points []) {}`. The mesh needs no record literal, so `zzAttr` is never registered. I derived this from the code and have not run it. Upstream Elm 0.19.1 has the same shape. Fix: register `attributes`/`uniforms` keys in the Shader arm (`Names.registerFieldList`). |

### Unhit but live (not dead)
- GenerateJs.Generate / Expression / Builder / Name / Mode / CodeGen.outputToString / GenerateJs.elm:31: the whole JS backend (globals, cycles, kernels incl. `Eco_` qualification, enums/boxes/ports/effect managers, the main-export trie, core-call inlining, if/case/tail-call/destructuring codegen, the Dev/Prod/Debug mode splits, the A2-A9/F2-F9 helpers, JS AST printing) is reachable via `--output=*.js|*.html`, `--optimize` and `--debug`, but untested.
- GenerateJs.SourceMap: all of it is reachable via `--sourcemaps`, but untested.
- Json.Decode: error branches (Expecting/Index/Field/Failure, oneOf failures, mapError), string escapes and the string/number syntax errors, and `false`/`null` literals are reachable from malformed or unusual elm.json/docs.json/registry/config JSON.
- Json.Encode: `write`/`writeUgly`/`encode`/`encodeUgly`, pretty array/object printing and the string escapes are reachable from init/install/bump/docs writing.
- Json.String: `fromComment` (minus item 18) is reachable via docs generation.
- Nitpick.Debug: all of `hasDebug` is reachable via `--optimize` (`checkForDebugUses`).
- Nitpick.Error: rendering of missing char/string/unit patterns and parenthesised ctor/cons patterns is reachable from incomplete `case` expressions.
- Docs: `fromModule` errors (ImplicitExposing/NoDocs/syntax/name/def problems), `@docs` operator parsing, `checkExport` (minus item 14), `gatherTypes`/`addDef`, the JSON encoders/decoders (docs.json, `eco diff`) and the Binop assoc decoder are reachable via package docs builds.
- Docs.Error: every report is reachable via a package with bad docs.
- Docs.Type: `toDoc`/`encode`/`fromRawType` (docs.json read and write) and `encodeMetadata`/`toTypeAliasField`/... are reachable via `--debug` metadata.
- Docs.Type.Extract: the `fromMsg`/`extractTransitive`/`fromDependencyInterface`/Extractor combinators and `mergeMany`/`merge` are reachable via `--debug` (Builder/Generate.elm:144-185, 502-520).
- Text.PrettyPrint.ANSI.Leijen: `displayIO`, the colour mapping and the SGR codes (minus item 20) are reachable by every coloured terminal report (Reporting/Doc.elm:164, Terminal/Terminal/Error.elm:102).
- Bytes codecs only used as MVar encoders/decoders: PatternMatches.elm:890-1063, Docs.Error.elm:307-563, Docs.elm:1059-1162, Docs.Type.elm:318-395, Extract.elm:460-475. They are reachable in the src-xhr (JS) build, where `Eco.MVar.put` serialises (compiler/src-xhr/Eco/MVar.elm:90-94), for any build with a pattern/docs error or a `--debug` build. Note: in the native build `eco-kernel-cpp/src/Eco/MVar.elm:70-72` ignores the encoder, so there they are effectively dead. This is cross-cutting (every MVar encoder), so it is a decision for the owner of Utils/MVar, not this batch.

## Area: b4-mlir-monoopt
Summary: 136 dead items (option 1: 38, option 2: 66, option 3: 15, option 4: 17), 0 claimed-unreachable-but-reachable.

Scope: Compiler.GenerateMlir.*, Mlir.*, Compiler.MonoOpt.*. All 804 unhit rows are covered (Expr 241; Patterns/Functions/Backend/Context 178; Intrinsics/Ops/Types/KernelAbi/TailRec/Lambdas/ConstThunks/LogicalTypes/TypeTable 136; BytesFusion 140; MonoOpt/Mlir 109). The unhit.csv row whose module is literally `module` is the CSV header and is ignored. Every existing `unreachable` exclusion and `crash` branch was re-checked; none was found reachable. Codegen restructurings were checked against design_docs/invariants.csv.

Notes on plan-appendix claims (plans/dead-code-claims-tidy-up.md) not covered by a table row:
- `Intrinsics.elm:575` (generateIntrinsicOp's StringOrderCompare/ValueEq/BoolEq arms dead, but wrong code if reached): confirmed, item 57.
- Plan appendix claim `Reify.elm:1260` (loop args bound as `[stepFnExpr, initialStateExpr]`, so DCountLoop/DSentinelLoop unreachable): STALE / already fixed. Reify.elm:1257 now binds `( "loop", [ initialStateExpr, stepFnExpr ] )`, which matches elm/bytes 1.0.8 `loop : state -> (state -> Decoder (Step state a)) -> Decoder a` (Decode.elm:412). Coverage confirms loop recognition runs: the only unhit rows in reifyLoop/matchCountStep/matchSentinelStep are the closure-arity fallbacks (item 87). The loop machinery in Reify/Emit/LoopIR is live; nothing to delete.

### Claimed-unreachable but reachable
none. Every existing `unreachable` exclusion and `crash` branch in these modules was checked and holds (some for a stronger, type-level reason than recorded; the option-2 items above say where the impossible state can be removed from the types).

### Unhit but live (not dead)

#### Expr
- Expr.elm:191, 215, 223, 235: SSA renaming of non-entry blocks, terminators and results, and `forceResultVar` when the name already matches. Generic helpers; reachable by let-bound case/closure shapes, untested.
- Expr.elm:363, 3629: registry lookup `Nothing` (`unknown_$_`, no core info). Registry totality (MONO_011) makes these plausibly dead but not shown for every caller; could join item 11's total-accessor change.
- Expr.elm:512 (MonoTailCall crash), 1640 (coerceResultToType crash), 5421 (duplicate let SSA crash), 6186 (jump index crash), 6375 (string fan-out crash): crash guards on invariants (tail calls only in TailRec-compiled positions; a coercible type pair; unique let names; jump table complete; one test kind per path) that this audit did not prove. Keep the crashes.
- Expr.elm:530: `tryInlinedDecodeFusion` succeeding on an inlined `Bytes.Decode.decode` let; reachable, untested.
- Expr.elm:613-614, 6632, 6635, 7134-7135, 7236: on the result spine, a tuple whose slot types differ from the sret layout, or a non-aggregate result. Plausibly dead if every spine expression has the function's return type, but not shown (MonoOpt may retype).
- Expr.elm:1297-1305: zero-arity closure (no captures, no params). Plausibly dead (closures have at least 1 param), but MonoOpt closure producers were not all checked.
- Expr.elm:1348: closure with a known `closureKind`, set by AbiCloning (AbiCloning.elm:1359-1563).
- Expr.elm:1425-1429, 1503, 1515, 1518, 1539, 1599, 1627-1631, 8069: boxing/coercion and intrinsic-gating corners (value aggregates under aggPromote, a primitive head type mismatch, other mismatches passed through).
- Expr.elm:1691, 1751, 1764, 1769, 2024: fast-dispatch stamp paths (AbiCloning stamps), plus a single-stage saturated closure call with no stamp.
- Expr.elm:1713, 2351, 2356, 2628-2629, 4348-4349, 4399-4400: calls with no arguments, or more arguments than the known stages. The code's comment cites an i1 Bool case.
- Expr.elm:1833: `"direct_flat"` reaches `applyByStages` only when a `CallDirectFlat` has a StageCurried callee (FloatMinMax.elm:124 sets `CallDirectFlat` on a default info). Reachable as far as shown.
- Expr.elm:2772, 2854, 2874, 2881, 2887: bytes-fusion let resolution (rebinding, lets found in inline bodies).
- Expr.elm:3334, 3347, 3360, 3363, 3517, 3543: `psplitArgFree` rejections, tuple3 aggregate projection, `ctorInline` off (non-default).
- Expr.elm:3954, 3961: `logBase` with boxed Float arguments.
- Expr.elm:4815, 5754, 5803, 5806, 5896: non-variable closure capture expressions (MonoOpt substitution and CafHoist may put non-locals there).
- Expr.elm:4963, 4976, 5319, 5380, 5533, 5726, 6029: let-group SCC windows, sibling/capture mapping misses, chain-head safety, restoring a shadowed destruct mapping.
- Expr.elm:5261: a destructuring path through `MonoField`.
- Expr.elm:6313: multi-result `eco.case` builder for a single-test chain on the spine.
- Expr.elm:7056, 7286, 7289, 7365, 7371, 7381, 7619, 7648, 7655, 7669, 7695, 7777, 7783, 7797, 7813, 7886: promotion escape-walk refusals and sret gating (data-dependent).

#### Patterns, Functions, Backend, Context
- Patterns.elm:330-334: `Elm_Kernel_Utils_equal` string test -- live under `valueEq = false` (non-default config, Config.elm:553).
- Patterns.elm:421-432: `materializeSplitParam` for a 3-tuple split and for a constructor split -- reachable via whole-value uses of a split name in Expr.elm:475/5523 (the 2-tuple arm is hit); untested shapes.
- Context.elm:777: gc-leaf upgrade of a kernel declaration first recorded by `registerKernelCall` (gcLeaf False) and later by `registerKernelInstance` with a gc-leaf-eligible KernelFacts row -- reachable, untested.
- Context.elm:783-784: kernel signature-mismatch crash -- a consistency guard between call-site types and instance ABIs; no argument either way that it is unreachable, so not claimed dead.
Notes on plan-appendix claims for this slice: Patterns.elm:1016/1034 confirmed (#29); Context.elm:555/569 confirmed (#50); CtorTag.elm:85 / Patterns.elm:1250 is already fixed (`isEmbeddedConstantCtor` checks the home module); Context.elm:402 (`oracleFacts`) is stale -- no such field remains in Context or Backend (only `MonoOpt.Borrow.deriveFacts`, slice D).

#### Intrinsics, Ops, Types, KernelAbi, TailRec, Lambdas, ConstThunks, LogicalTypes, TypeTable
- ConstThunks.elm:221 `Refuse "notThunk"` -- reachable when a nullary def aliases `main` (`foo = main`) or a nullary non-MonoDefine node: `isNullaryTarget` checks only the signature, `isCandidate` also excludes main; untested.
- ConstThunks.elm:411 `pureCallee` global arm `_ -> False` -- reachable for an accessor callee (`x = .a { a = 1 }`, Accessor spec key) or a node without signature; untested.
- Lambdas.elm:235 `parseNumericIndex` `Nothing` -- non-numeric sibling SSA names (e.g. `%decider`, param names) are possible; untested.
- Ops.elm:861-880 `ecoToHeap`/`ecoFromHeap` -- emitted by Expr.boxToEcoValue / coerceResultToType when a value aggregate (sret/psplit tuple or custom) meets a boxed context; plausible, not proven dead (depends on Expr-slice analysis of those aggregate branches).
- TailRec.elm:734 `ecoProjectTuple3Agg` in `projectSlotsFromAgg` -- a split 3-tuple parameter given an aggregate-valued tail-call argument; reachable, untested.
- TailRec.elm:916 pass-through slot-type mismatch `Nothing` -- defensive; `splitAggParams` may hold psplit-worker params whose slot plan comes from a different planner (Functions.elm:1177), not proven equal.
- TailRec.elm:1660 `Nothing -> acc` -- names already in `currentLetSiblings` get no new mapping from `addPlaceholderMappings` (Expr.elm:4748-4750); probably always in varMappings, not proven.
- TailRec.elm:1776 destructor-type fallback -- reachable when the path type still has a type variable but the destructor type is concrete.
- Types.elm:311-339 `mlirTypeToString` -- used only to format crash messages (Context.insertKernelDecl, Expr.coerceResultToType, TailRec.checkedYieldOperands, KernelAbi); lives exactly as long as those crashes; keep as diagnostic helper.

#### BytesFusion
- Reify encoder recogniser fall-throughs (274, 288, 291, 310, 313, 325, 341, 643): an accessor or other non-Global callee (`.enc r`), a curried or let-bound call of a non-`Bytes.Encode` function, or a `let` binding a local tail function. Reachable by user encoders of other shapes; untested.
- Reify decoder recogniser fall-throughs (1084, 1098, 1101, 1107, 1120, 1123, 1135, 1147, 1463): the same shapes for decoders, plus `andThen` with a body that calls `Bytes.Decode` but is not `string`/`bytes`. Untested.
- `List.cons` / `List.map` / `List.length` global fall-throughs (514-518, 540, 562): a non-core package `List` module, or `List.cons` surviving as a global rather than the kernel. Not provably excluded (it depends on how `::` is lowered); untested.
- reifyEndianness zero-argument call arm (913): accepts `MonoCall fn []`. No producer was found, but there is no proof that none exists; it is a speculative accept.
- map5 decoding (Reify 1247, 1413, 2459-2485 map5 helper, 2737-2753 DMap5 compile; Emit 1165 Apply5): `Bytes.Decode.map5` is a real API (Decode.elm:262), and no test uses it.
- Inlined-elm/bytes-shape matchers (2048, 2051, 2105, 2108, 2173, 2176, 2179, 2260-2278, 2292): recognising elm/bytes internals after inlining. The fall-throughs depend on optimiser output shape (e.g. a Decoder ctor arg that is a partial application, `Decoder (loopHelp ..)`, or a closure split by staging), so there is no proof they are dead.
- Small matchers (2325 curried flatCall, 2354 isLocalNamedVia, 2392 isBytesDecodeGlobal non-Global, 2413 isCoreFunction non-Global): ordinary negative cases.
- resolveLocalHelp fuel exhaustion (1633): needs a chain of more than 32 let aliases. It is a termination guard; keep it.
- Emit monoExprType MonoDestruct/MonoAccessorValue arms (1878, 1899): reachable (`D.map .field dec`); they go away with item 95.

#### MonoOpt and Mlir
- `MonoAccessorValue` arms (AbiCloning 484, 973, 1153, 3265; GlobalOptimize 689; InlineSimplify 2492, 3576, 4433, 4793, 5881): accessor placeholders that ResolveAccessorValues cannot resolve stay in the graph (produced at Solver/Translate.elm:894, Subst/Specialize.elm:3716); untested.
- AbiCloning rare stamping/census outcomes: second layout group (609), `srcLambda` identity (664), `lssBlockedMembers` (716), graph with no closures or origins (723), `LPartial` callee sets (1633, 1634, 1639; produced by the solver, Solver/Monomorphize.elm:338), callee shapes closureLiteral/case/other (2100, 2103, 2112), staged/flattened/pap scan declines and fall-through (2246, 2251, 2254, 2260, 2263, 2320, 2345, 2391, 2394, 2400), unpeelable / no-spec pap and post-settle declines (2590, 2644, 2712), unboxed-capture flag in fingerprints (3039).
- GlobalOptimize: extern arity (906), closure-of-case arity (1156), partial-stage peel (1290), generic apply for polymorphic return (1428): reachable shapes, untested.
- InlineSimplify: alias-arity chains (708, 711, 719, 2650), case root/label equal to the forwarded name (4184), `calleeArity` of other callees (4244, e.g. a local-variable callee at :2986), tail call to a counted name (5900).
- InlineSimplify shadowing / name-clash guards (3584, 3632, 3647, 3702, 4146, 4166, 4201, 4800, 4847, 4859, 4906, 5920, 5963): the exclusions argue Elm forbids shadowing and inline copies are freshened (freshenLetBoundNames). That is plausible but not proven for compiler-generated binders (destructuring temporaries, accessor `record` params, etc.), and the guards are the correct capture-avoiding semantics; keep them. Note the appendix bug InlineSimplify.elm:5925 (countUsages' MonoDestruct arm does not stop at a shadowing binder) is in this same family.
- InlineSimplify 2169 (loopify capture filter `_ -> False`) and 4256 (`calleeArity` of a closure literal): exclusions rely on "captures are always local variables" and "closure-literal callees are beta-reduced first", but capture expressions are rewritten by `rewriteCaptures` (:3036) and `simplifyLetsCaptures` (:5411), and the ordering argument is about one rewrite path only; not proven, keep.
Notes on other inputs: `AbiCloning.peelStages` export (elm-review prod-only) is used internally (:1674, :2318) and its export only by AbiCloningFlatPeelTest: not dead. Plan-appendix claims GlobalOptimize.elm:741 (rewriteExprForAbi family) and InlineSimplify.elm:5866/6028 (inlineVar family) are stale: none of those functions exist any more. VarInt.elm:203 (shiftRightBy) is a latent defect, not dead code.

## Area: b5-mono-premono
Summary: 113 dead items (option 1: 29, option 2: 37, option 3: 32, option 4: 8, mixed: 7 [1/3: 4, 3/4: 2, 2/3: 1]), 5 claimed-unreachable-but-reachable.

Scope notes:
- Default engine: Solver (`Config.default`, src/Compiler/Config.elm:541, `engine = EngineSolver`). Subst and Diff are selected only by `ECO_MONO_ENGINE` or the config file's `mono.engine` (src/Builder/Eco/Config.elm:1179; dispatch in src/Compiler/Pipeline/Steps.elm:271-280). They are flag-gated and live, not dead. The only unreferenced Subst code is its two convenience wrappers (item 1). Solver's equivalents are item 28.
- Some proposals recur in more than one item, because each sub-table lists the arms in its own module. Each such proposal is one change:
  - Removing `TOpt.Kernel` (and, in Translate, `TOpt.ArrayIndex`/`HintUnknown`): items 6, 42, 67, 91.
  - The `withNumberMulti`/`withLocalMulti` bracketing: items 44, 69.
  - Deleting the kernel tunnels (plan claims 131, 132, 134): items 70, 78.
  - Removing untracked `Function`/`Define`/`VarLocal`/`Record` from the typed IR: item 19, which also covers items 50, 60, 65, 89, 90, 94.
- Plan claims (plans/dead-code-claims-tidy-up.md):
  - Lines 130, 131/132, 133, 134, 135 and 136 are confirmed.
  - Line 73 is stale: there is no duplicate `renderFailure` any more.
  - Line 122 is fixed by item 21.
  - The line numbers in the plan for Solver/Monomorphize are stale. Use the locations below.
- If items 70/78 land, update the invariants.csv row LSS_021 and the KernelSetFacts.elm:138 docs: both still describe a `PSFTunnels` constructor that no longer exists.
- A side finding, not dead code: a let-bound number list destructured in a `case` and then used at both Int and Float crashes the default compiler with "Kernel signature mismatch for Elm_Kernel_Basics_mul_Float". The repro is /tmp/deadcode/scratch/b5-mono-premono/port1/src/NumList2.elm.

### Claimed-unreachable but reachable
| Location | Claim | Reaching input |
|---|---|---|
| src/Compiler/Monomorphize/ResolveAccessorValues.elm:228-231 (row 231, `Nothing -> accDict` in `joinValueInfo`) | exclusion: "joinValueInfo joins two branches of one type, so both records hold the same fields and VI_Record keeps every field" | The reasoning is wrong. A *joined* `VI_Record` drops every field whose join is `VI_Unknown` (220-226). `List.foldl joinValueInfo` (558, 586) then passes that partial record as `r2` against a fresh full record `r1`. Example: `type alias R = { h : Int -> Int }` and `pick n = if n == 0 then { f = .h, g = 1 } else if n == 1 then { f = .h, g = 2 } else { f = .h, g = 3 }`. `.h` returns a function, so `accessorTypeNeedsDefer` keeps it as a `MonoAccessorValue` (`VI_Accessor`), while `g` is `VI_Unknown`. The first join gives `{f}`, and the second looks up `g` in `{f}`, which misses. The behaviour is correct: the field is dropped either way. Only the exclusion's reason is wrong. Derived from the code, not run |
| src/Compiler/Monomorphize/Solver/Monomorphize.elm:4651-4652 (`ctorBackedGlobal` `fuel <= 0 -> False`; exclusion 4527) | "only Link steps consume fuel... one step" | Wrong reasoning: the `TrackedDefine` alias arms (4671/4674, which are hit) also consume fuel. With `inline.aliasForward = false` (a config option; AliasForward also leaves alias definitions unrewritten), a chain of 9 or more top-level aliases ending at a constructor (`a1 = Just`, `a2 = a1`, ..., `a9 = a8`) with `a9` used as a function value in a lambda set runs out of fuel. The member then gets `OriginGlobal` instead of `OriginCtor`. This is benign (a conservative label), and with the default `aliasForward = True` the references are forwarded to `Just`. Fix the exclusion's reason. Not executed. |
| src/Compiler/Monomorphize/Solver/Translate.elm:1147 (`connectEncoderType` `_ -> s0`) | "an outgoing port's type is always a TLambda" | `type alias Out msg = Int -> Cmd msg` + `port echoOut : Out msg` (used once). Verified: the coverage build records a hit on 1147 Effect: the port node's type is the annotation as written (LocalOpt/Typed/Module.elm:430-443), so it is a `TAlias`; the encoder is not connected. No miscompile seen (encoders for valid payloads are already function-typed); fix: unwrap aliases (or delete `connectEncoderType` if the encoder type is always concrete) and drop the exclusion |
| src/Compiler/Monomorphize/Solver/Translate.elm:1217 (`recordPort` duplicate skip) | "a port is specialized once per spec of its global ... would need two types for one monomorphic port" | `port send : String -> Cmd msg` used as `Cmd Int` and as `Cmd String` (two specs `PortTwice_send_$_12/_14`). Verified hit on 1217 Effect: ports are polymorphic in `msg`; the dedupe is correct behaviour, only the exclusion reason is wrong. Drop the exclusion |
| src/Compiler/Monomorphize/Solver/Zonk.elm:141-147 (row 147, `_ -> Dict.empty`) | exclusion: "a parameter used as a record extension is filled only by a record type, so the binding is always an MRecord" | The container's type argument can be a residual `MVar _ CEcoValue` (MONO_028 keeps residuals), and `instantiateUnionType` (Translate.elm:7215) binds the parameter to it. Example: `type W a = W { a \| x : Int }`, `g : W a -> Int`, `g (W r) = r.x`, `main = Html.text (String.fromInt (List.length (List.map g [])))`. Here g's spec has `a` residual, and destructuring `W r` goes through `computeCustomFieldType` to `canTypeToMonoWithI` with the extension bound to `MVar`, which takes this arm. This is from reading the code; I did not run it. The result (a closed `{x : Int}`) looks benign, because no such value exists at runtime, but the exclusion's reasoning is wrong and should be reworded, not trusted. |

### Unhit but live (not dead)
#### Shared Monomorphize helpers, Pipeline, PreMono
- Pipeline.Steps, every row (115, 196, 207, 240-241, 280, 307-312, 350, 354, 362, 380, 397, 417-466, 530, 537, 571, 578, 597, 629-634): non-default config only. `mono.engine = subst/diff`, `inline.aliasForward/etaExpand/postMono/pruneDead` off, `mono.validate`, `list.chunks` off or `list.mapTemplate` on, `cse.report/enabled`, `cafMemo.dedupe/census/hoist.enabled`.
- Engine choice: Solver is the default (`Config.elm:541`, `engine = EngineSolver`). Subst and Diff are selected only by `ECO_MONO_ENGINE` or the config's `mono.engine` (Builder/Eco/Config.elm:1179, Steps.elm:271-280), so they are flag-gated and live. Only the two Subst wrappers above are unreferenced. The coverage filter already dropped the Subst modules' rows.
- TypeSubst.elm:788, 799 (`resolveMonoVarsHelp` record/custom "changed" arms): a substitution binding that contains another bound variable inside a record or custom type. Common in the flag-gated Subst engine's unifier, and untested.
- Prune.elm:112 (`markReachable` with a missing edge row): no argument that every reachable spec has a `Just` edge row (`collectSpecEdges` gives `Nothing` for an empty node slot). Untested.
- `Holey` alias arms in the pre-mono passes: AliasForward.elm:285, EtaExpand.elm:1799-1838 (`unAlias` Holey arm and the whole `substTypeVars`), 1892, Fresh.elm:313. Probably unreached because meta types come from the solver (`Filled`), but AssignMVarIds.rewriteAliasType's `Holey` arm is executed, so `Holey` types do reach the typed graph. Not proven dead.
- EtaExpand.elm:266 (non-empty `etaOnly` allow-list, a flag). 1349: an over-applied global inside a cheap call (e.g. `apply f x y` with `apply : (a -> b) -> a -> b`), reachable and untested. 1931/1943 (`calleeArity` for an unindexed global or a non-global callee): no argument found.
- Fresh.elm copyExpr arms (440-449, 469-481, 546, 709-713, 742-748): reached only through `Fresh.freshenCopy` from the pre-mono inliner (`inline.preMono`, default off). The 1537-1594 `checkType`/`checkExpr` lambdas belong to `assertMinted` (`mono.validate`).
- Test-only exports that production uses inside their own module (not dead): Prune.restrictToSccEdges (Prune.elm:172), AliasForward.Target(..)/aliasMap, Steps.assignFlagsFor/monomorphizeEntry.
- Builder/Generate.elm:100-101 imports `Solver.Monomorphize` and `Subst.Monomorphize` but uses neither (another batch's module, noted for completeness).
#### Solver.Monomorphize + Solver.Engine
- Engine report and census counters: `bumpWidenedByCf`, `bumpArgFlowCensusBy`, `bumpAppliedArrow` and similar (Engine.elm:988-1163), and the scratch-store census in `withScratchStore` (2185-2208). These run only under `lss.report`/`lss.qCensus`, a non-default config.
- `renderLssReport` internals (Monomorphize.elm:1497-3562; rows 1570-3544): `lss.report` only.
- Engine LSS-off `enqueueSpec` arm (Engine.elm:2242-2262, beyond the constant logic listed above) and processItem's `completionJoin = Nothing` path (Monomorphize.elm:4006). These are live under `lss.enabled = false` or EngineDiff.
- LSS settle-pass lattice edge cases: settleVarLambda cell misses and sets-free cells (815/818); varSuccPass skips and `c\|` members, plus the list/tuple/record rewrites (1284, 1297, 1339, 1381-1425); `recordLambdaHome` arity conflict (1089); `varCellWalk` `LPartial` (1173); settleVarCtorRows `LPartial` contamination (511). All are reachable by programs with the matching lambda-set shapes, but untested.
- `mergeConvergedSpecs` port remapping (286, 289, 323): reachable by a program whose port decoder specs converge.
- Engine μ-tie and fold paths: `recordMuTied` (whole decl, 942-951; caller 799), root-fold source insert (839), the SpecId-fallback layout key (886), and `lambdaInstanceMemberId` with no current spec (737-741; possible for mints from `stampSelfSpine` at seed time, unproven). These are LSS paths with no test that reaches them.

- `buildMemberOrigins` arms where a member's recorded source does not match its key prefix (Monomorphize.elm:4593, 4601, 4609, 4620). A member can be minted without a recorded source (sources are inserted separately, e.g. Engine.elm:835-841), so these arms are not provably dead.
- `sameGlobalAncestors` fuel exhaustion (Engine.elm:1335): reachable only with spec chains longer than 100000 specs. It is a defensive bound.
- Internal-error watchdogs and EngineBug reporting, kept as is (option 4 / keep crash). These cover the LSS_010 join-flush watchdog (Monomorphize.elm:3756-3778, including the 3772 lookup arm), the MONO_029 saturation watchdog (4088), the `Err` propagations (3795, 3930), the accessor `EngineBug` (3901; dead by callers, because Translate.elm:894-898 enqueues an `Accessor` only after `accessorTypeNeedsDefer` checked `MFunction (MRecord :: _)` with a non-function result, and Zonk builds arrows curried one argument at a time, but it is already an `Err`, not a crash), and `renderFailure` `UnifyMismatch`/`EngineBug` with `crashFailure` (Engine.elm:1819-1838). None of these can be removed without proving termination or monotonicity.
- `monomorphizeWithReportAssigned` "No main function found" (Monomorphize.elm:151): Terminal/Make.elm `getNoMains` rejects roots without `main`, and the test pipeline adds one. It is a non-crash `Err` shared with Subst, so keep it.

- LssInfer.elm:198, 228, `srcLam = Nothing` in injectLambdaMember / injectLambdaMemberQualifiedId (existing exclusions): invariant-unreachable. - LssInfer.elm:3091: directChildren's `VarDebug` arm. walkExpr and collectReferencedGlobals do not match VarDebug, so a `Debug.*` reference that is not a callee reaches it.
- KernelSetFacts `TypeShape(..)` constructors (elm-review prod-only): used by the module's own TransportsAs rows (1291, 1316). Only the `(..)` export is test-only, so this is not dead.

| C-R1 | src/Compiler/Canonicalize/Environment/Foreign.elm:154 | `crash "kernel imports cannot use `as`"`, a direct crash that assumes the case cannot occur | A module in a kernel project (a package named `elm/*`, `elm-explorations/*` or `eco/*`, or a kernel application, per `Pkg.isKernel` and `Parse.isKernel`) containing `import Elm.Kernel.List as L` while `List.js` exists. Builder.Build:968 resolves it as a kernel import (`checkKernelExistsInDirs`). `toSafeImports` then calls `isNormal`, which crashes the compiler. Upstream Elm has the same `error`. | R1 | src/Compiler/Type/Type.elm:1017 (excluded=crash) | `crash "Used toErrorType on a type that is not well-formed"` is impossible | REPRODUCED with build/compiler/build-xhr/bin/guida.js: `f : { a \| x : Int } -> a -> Int` / `f r y = (y + 1) + (r + 1)`. `y + 1` fails (rigid `a` vs number) and the failed unify turns `a`'s class into `Error`; the later `r + 1` mismatch renders `r`'s type, whose extension is now `ET.Error`, and hits the `_ -> crash`. | Compiler crash instead of two type errors (any user program). Plan appendix claims in these modules: the Details.elm:2388 claim is confirmed (item 4). The CtorTag.elm:85 entry (True/False-named constructors tagged 0xFFFD) is about correctness, not dead code, and none of its lines are unhit.

### Unhit but live (not dead)
- Builder.Eco.Config (all apply*Override, loadBase, update*/set*, finishWithWarnings): reached through `eco-config.json` and `ECO_*` environment overrides, which no test sets.
- Builder.Eco.FEStats (every row): reached under `eco make --stats` (Terminal/Make.elm:181), which no test passes.
- Builder.Deps.Details package build path (verifyInstall/verifyPkg/verifyApp/verifyConstraints, download, crawl*, compile*, writePackageArtifacts, gather*, docs, `std*` helpers, cache checks, local-package refresh) and its in-memory codecs (status/dResult/statusDict/docsStatus/validOutline/artifact caches), plus `detailsDecoder` for incremental builds: reachable when installing or building packages and when d.dat is reused. The tests run offline on prebuilt packages.
- Builder.Deps.Solver, Registry, Diff, Bump, Website: package solving and fetching and the `install`/`diff`/`bump`/`publish` commands, which need the network.
- Compiler.IR.Optimized (every node/expr/def/decider/choice/path/effects/main codec, `compareGlobal`, `ecoKernelHome`), DecisionTree.Path codec, Kernel.Chunk (`addField`, chunk codec): the erased JavaScript backend (`Opt.localGraphEncoder` in Build.elm, GenerateJs/Generate.elm:454/679/745, GraphAssembly). The JIT E2E suite does not use it.
- Compiler.IR.Canonical expr/pattern/def codecs: reached when a `RecordUpdateKeys` type error, which carries `Can.FieldUpdate` expressions (Type/Error.elm:3219-3222), is serialized. Any expression form can appear as a field value. The collectStringsFromType/AliasType arms come from extensible records and holey aliases in stored types.
- Compiler.IR.Canonical.Interface `ExportEverything`/binop branches: a module with `exposing (..)` or infix declarations compiled through `fromModule`.
- Compiler.IR.Source (comment combinators c1map/c2map/…, fComment/manager/effects/infix/shader codecs, `getName` Main), Source.Shader (escape/unescape/type codec), Source.Binop: formatter, effect-manager, port and GLSL inputs.
- Decoder `Bytes.Decode.fail` default arms reading on-disk formats (Interface, TOpt typed artifacts, TypedPath, Source, Shader, d.dat, artifacts.dat): reached by corrupt or old cache files. The fail arms of the MVar-only codecs in Details and Canonical could only be hit by in-memory corruption; leave them as they are (Bytes.Decode needs a result).
- Compiler.IR.Mono lattice and LSS corners (topOfKind/topKindLabel codes 1, 4, 6-9, 14 and legacy; LPartial arms in enrichAnno/collectAnnoGo/hasVarAnno/annoCoverage/toComparableFragments; isVarAnno; join/overlayAnnotationsChanged no-change arms; unionAnno LVar; joinCollisionCells; recoverStoredSets; eqLayoutList unequal lengths; shallowLayoutKey number var; clearLssTables `keepOrigins` false): producers exist (IO.lsTop* codes 0-20, `unionAnno` builds `LPartial`, borrow/map-template config flags). These are untested corners of the lambda-set lattice.
- `Mono.stageParamTypes`/`stageReturnType` non-function arms: the GlobalOptimize caller has already matched `MFunction`, but the `Staging.checkClosureStaging` validator can pass a malformed type. They are defensive arms.
- Compiler.IR.Mono.Traverse (mapNodeTypes for Enum/ManagerLeaf/Port, MonoAccessorValue, DtUnbox, capture/rename arms), Mono.Closure.extractRegion arms, Mono.Intern equality and widen arms, Mono.Registry miss arms, Mono.Abi `MVar CNumber`: generic traversals over constructors that do exist (ports, effect managers and so on), or arms whose freedom from residual number variables rests only on an invariant.
- Kernel.Intrinsics remaining `Nothing` fallbacks (bitwise, utils append mixed, JsArray decline paths, boxedComparable False, concreteArraySet MVar, arrayElementType Array): reached by kernel calls at types the intrinsic does not cover. Kernel.Facts `hoistable`/`splitSymbol` Nothing: the CSE path and non-kernel symbols.
- Typed.Optimized `TailDef` with a `Nothing` tvar (codec arms), `formatVersionDecoder` fail, `pathDecoderS`/`deciderDecoderS`/`choiceDecoderS`/`exprDecoderS` fail; TypeTable 4-byte widths (more than 65536 types) and `entryDecoder` fail; Canonical.Alias `dealiasHelp` TUnit; Compiler.IR.Typed.TypeTable `widthFor` 4.
- elm-review prod-only export hits that are used inside their own module (`annoCovers`, `eqKeySpec`, `isTopAnno`, `layoutHashOf`, `topLegacy`, `topPoison`, `refMaybe`, `rows`, Interface `Union`/`Alias` constructors, `NodeTypes`): only the export list is unneeded; the code is live.

## Area: b8-rest
Summary: 17 dead items (option 1: 4, option 2: 7, option 3: 2, option 4/keep crash: 4), 3 claimed-unreachable-but-reachable.

Context: `unreachable-decls.csv` is empty for this batch. Every top-level declaration here survives `elm make` tree shaking from `Terminal.Main` or `API.Main`, so nothing is dead at declaration level. The dead code is constructors, branches and one dependency. `Terminal.Main` wires `init`, `make`, `install`, `uninstall`, `bump` and `diff`, plus two `disabled` slots. `API.Main` wires `make`, `format`, `install`, `uninstall` and `diagnostics` (content or path). All of the command modules behind these are reachable.

### Claimed-unreachable but reachable
| # | Location | Claim | Input that reaches it |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | src/API/Make.elm:105-106 | `crash "No main!"` (author-marked crash) | The API with `{"command":"diagnostics","path":"src/Foo.elm"}`, or `{"command":"make",...}`, where `Foo` is a module without `main`, e.g. any library or helper module. The API process crashes instead of writing a JSON error. Fix together with #7: return `Err Exit.MakeNoMain...` instead. |
| R2 | src/Builder/Build.elm:3030-3031 | `addInside`'s `RNotFound` crash ("Error from `X` should have been reported already") | Found by reading, not run. In an application that depends directly on `elm/html`, create `src/Html.elm` and run `eco make src/Html.elm`. `getRootInfoHelp` accepts the path as `LInside "Html"`. `crawlModule` → `crawlSinglePath` finds `Html` in `foreigns` and returns `SBadImport (Import.Ambiguous ...)`. `checkModule` turns that into `RNotFound`. No module imports the root, so no `RProblem` carries the error, `gatherProblemsOrMains` returns `Ok`, and `addInside` crashes. Fix: report root ambiguity as a `BP_*` problem in `getRootInfoHelp`/`checkRoots`, or have `addErrors` turn a root's `RNotFound` into an error. |
| R3 | src/Builder/Build.elm:3036-3037 (`RBlocked`), 3002-3003 (`ROutsideBlocked`, "eco-stuff/ is corrupted") | Author-marked crashes | Build once, then delete or corrupt a cached dependency's `.eci` in the stuff dir, touch a module that imports it (or an outside root), and rebuild. `loadInterface` → `File.readBinary` gives `Nothing` → `Corrupted`. `loadInterfaces` then gives `Nothing`, and `compileIfInterfacesLoaded`/`recompileIfInterfacesLoaded`/`checkRoot` give `RBlocked`/`ROutsideBlocked` with no error recorded, so the build crashes. The docstring at Build.elm:3013-3016 admits this. It should be reported (e.g. reuse `Exit.GenerateCannotLoadArtifacts`'s corrupt-cache report, or recompile the dependency). |

Every constructor except #3 and #5 has a producer (checked by script for all Exit constructors). Error paths, untested.
- Builder.Reporting.Exit.JsonError: elm.json parse and decode error reports (bad JSON, wrong types), reachable by a malformed elm.json. Exit.elm:2410's `\_ _ _ _ -> never` is a `Never` eliminator; it cannot run, and nothing needs doing.
- Builder.Reporting and Exit.Help: JSON/silent reporting styles, download/build progress, y/n prompts, non-terminal output. The appendix claim for Help.elm:204 is stale: `toHandle` now passes the handle to `IO.isTerminal` (Help.elm:200), so the plain-text branch is reachable.
- Builder.Build: package builds (`fromExposed`, docs), outside roots, cached-module reuse, import cycles and ambiguous imports, root-path problems, kernel-application env (`--kernel-package`), absolute source dirs, and the BResult/Status/Root codecs (xhr build). Error and incremental paths, untested.
- Builder.Generate: the JS debug/dev/prod paths, source maps, Debug-remnant check, cached-object loading, `--text-mlir` streaming writer, monomorphization error throws, and GlobalOpt/census report writers enabled by eco-config report options.
- Builder.Elm.Outline, File, Stuff, Http, GcPoints and GraphAssembly: outline encode/validate errors, package write/copy, custom ELM_HOME, bundled packages, archive download, GC-points pre-link (ELF), local kernel chunks.
- Builder.Reporting.ModuleError: multi-module error ordering and JSON encoding.
- Utils.Main, Utils.Bytes.*, Utils.Task.Extra, Data.Map/Set/Map.Utils/NonEmptyList/OneOrMore, Codec.Archive.Zip, System.IO: generic helpers whose unhit callers are live, plus filepath edge cases. The `find`/`dictFind`/`mapFindMin`/`foldl1`/`foldr1` crashes are generic partial lookups whose callers (many outside this batch) were not all audited. Not proven dead; keep.
- Data.Int64 `hostIs64` True branches of `toWords`/`fromWords`: native host only (coverage ran on JS). Exporting `fromWords`/`hostIs64` is needed only by tests/Data/Int64Test.elm. Both are used inside the module, so this is not dead code.
- Data.BitSet `insertGrowing`/`removeGrowing` negative-index guards, `growTo`'s no-extra-words branch, `setWord` bounds check, and Data.HashMap `remove` of an absent key: defensive library guards that valid inputs can reach.
- Builder.BackgroundWriter:58: a unit encoder used only by the src-xhr MVar path.
